Denote by
p_{1}:\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}^{\mathrm{act},\rightarrow}\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
the morphism sending
[k]\overset{b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n]
to
[k]
, then this category is isomorphic to the fiber
p_{1}^{-1}([m])
. Given an active morphism
[k]\overset{b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[m]
, the induced morphism
b:(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m}\rightarrow(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{k}
sends
([m]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[l]\xrightarrow{f}[n])
to
([k]\overset{a\circ b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[l]\xrightarrow{f}[n])
. Similarly, for an inert morphism
i:[s]\rightarrowtail[m]
the induced morphism sends it to
([m]\overset{\widetilde{a}}{\twoheadrightarrow}[l]\xrightarrow{f\circ\widetilde{i}}[n])
, where
a\circ i\cong\widetilde{i}\circ\widetilde{a}
is the active/inert decomposition in
\Delta
. Generally we will denote by
f^{*}:p_{1}^{-1}([m])\rightarrow p_{1}^{-1}([l])
the induced functor for a morphism
f:[l]\rightarrow[m]
in
\Delta
.

Now assume that we are given a Segal
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
-space
\mathscr{F}
, we will associate to it a functor
L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n]\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}
. First, observe that using
lemma

5.16
and
remark

5.13
we can view
\mathscr{F}
as a presheaf on
((\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U})^{\mathrm{op}}
. Next, observe that a functor
(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m}\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{m}\cong\mathrm{Seg}_{\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]}}(\mathscr{S})
is an element of

(6)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m},\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]},\mathscr{S}))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m}\times\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]},\mathscr{S}).

Denote by
\overline{p}
the composition
p_{1}\circ\widehat{p}:(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}^{\mathrm{act},\rightarrow})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
, then it follows from
lemma

5.15
that

(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m}\times\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]})\cong(p_{1}^{-1}([m])\times\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]})\cong\overline{p}^{-1}([m]).

For each
m
we then define the required morphism
(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{m}\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{m}
to be given by the restriction of
\mathscr{F}
to
\overline{p}^{-1}([m])
(the fact that this functor lands in
\mathrm{Corr}_{m}
follows from
remark

5.17
). We then send every morphism
f:[l]\rightarrow[m]
in
\Delta
to
f^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}:\mathrm{Corr}_{m}\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{l}
.

For this definition to be consistent we need to prove that for every morphism
f
as above the following diagram commutes

\hbox to147.2pt{\vbox to72.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 73.59962pt\lower-35.95952pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-73.59962pt}{-29.79979pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{

Now fix an object
([k]\overset{b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])\in\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{act},\rightarrow}_{/[n]}
. Assume we are given an active morphism
a:[l]\twoheadrightarrow[k]
in
\Delta
, then we need to prove that

\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([l]\overset{b\circ a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])}}\cong a^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{({[f]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n]})}},

however this follows from

\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([l]\overset{b\circ a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])}}\cong b^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}(a^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([s]=\joinrel=[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])})}\cong a^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([f]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])}}.

Now assume that we are given an inert morphism
i:[t]\rightarrowtail[k]
, consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[k]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[s]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[l]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[t]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

obtained from the active/inert factorization in
\Delta
. We need to prove that

\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([l]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[t]\xrightarrow{g\circ j}[n])}}\cong i^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([k]\overset{b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])}},

which follows from
remark

5.13
,
lemma

5.16
and the following string of isomorphisms

\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([l]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[t]\xrightarrow{g\circ j}[n])}}\cong a^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}(j^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([s]=\joinrel=[s]\xrightarrow{g\circ j}[n])}})\cong i^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}(b^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([s]=\joinrel=[s]\xrightarrow{g\circ j}[n])}})\cong i^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}|_{\widehat{p}_{([k]\overset{b}{\twoheadrightarrow}[s]\xrightarrow{g}[n])}}.

This defines a morphism

H:(\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\sim}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n],\mathrm{Corr})^{\sim}.

Now assume that we are given a functor
F:L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n]\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}
, we will construct from it an object of
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
which we identify with a functor
\mathscr{F}:(\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
. First we define it on
\coprod_{m\geq 0}\overline{p}^{-1}([m])
using
eq.

6
above. Observe that, since
F
was assumed to be a functor to
\mathrm{Corr}
,
\mathscr{F}
satisfies the conditions of both
lemma

5.16
and
remark

5.13
, so the only thing we need to do is to extend it to
(\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}
. For a morphism
f:[l]\rightarrow[k]
in
\Delta
we can view
\overline{p}^{-1}(f)
as a correspondence from
\overline{p}^{-1}([k])
to
\overline{p}^{-1}([l])
which we will denote by
f^{-1}
. It follows from our previous considerations that
f^{-1}
is isomorphic to

(f^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}},f^{*}):\mathrm{Corr}_{k}\times p_{1}^{-1}([k])\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{l}\times p_{1}^{-1}([l]).

In particular, it follows that
(g\circ f)^{-1}\mathscr{F}\cong f^{-1}\circ g^{-1}\mathscr{F}
for any
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
. Fix an object
(x,y)\in\mathrm{Corr}_{l}\times p_{1}^{-1}([l])
, then we see that

f^{-1}\mathscr{F}(x,y)\cong\underset{(f^{*}t\rightarrow y)\in f^{*}/y}{\mathrm{colim}}f^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}(x,t)\cong\underset{(f^{*}t\rightarrow y)\in f^{*}/y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x,f^{*}t),

where the second isomorphism follows from the fact that
F
was assumed to be a functor to
\mathrm{Corr}
.

Observe that we have

(\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}\cong\underset{g:[m]\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\overline{p}^{-1}(g),

so it suffices to extend
\mathscr{F}
to various
\overline{p}^{-1}(g)
in a compatible way. Recall from
3.23
that in order to extend
\mathscr{F}
to
\overline{p}^{-1}(g)
we need to define a section
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{inj}}_{/[m]}\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathscr{F}}_{m}
. Observe that, since
s
is already defined on morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\infty,\mathrm{inj}}_{/[m]}
, it suffices to define it for inert morphism that preserve the minimal element. First, assume that we are given a morphism
[1]\xrightarrow{[s,t]}[m]
whose image in
\Delta
is given by
[a]\xrightarrow{g}[b]
and let
i:[0]\rightarrowtail[1]
be the inert morphism given by the inclusion of
\{0\}
. To this data we need to associate a morphism
\alpha_{g}:g^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{[b]}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{[a]}
, where
\mathscr{F}_{[a]}
and
\mathscr{F}_{[b]}
denote the restrictions of
\mathscr{F}
to the fibers over
[a]
and
[b]
. We define it as

g^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{[b]}(x,y)\cong\underset{(g^{*}t\rightarrow y)\in g^{*}/y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}_{[a]}(x,g^{*}t)\rightarrow\underset{(w\rightarrow y)\in p_{1}^{-1}([a])/y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}_{[a]}(x,w)\cong\mathrm{id}_{!}\mathscr{F}_{[a]}(x,y)\cong\mathscr{F}_{[a]}(x,y),

where the morphism of colimits is induced by the natural inclusion between the underlying diagrams.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{21.44144pt}{2.34387pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Denote by
(x_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}x_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{2}}...\xrightarrow{g_{l}}x_{l})
the image of
[l]
in
(\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}
, then we define the required morphism as

\displaystyle g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}^{-1}...g_{l}^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{l}\cong

\displaystyle g_{1}^{-1}...g_{l^{\prime}}^{-1}(g_{l}\circ...\circ g_{l^{\prime}+1})^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{l}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{g_{l}\circ...\circ g_{l^{\prime}+1}}}g_{1}^{-1}...g_{l^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathscr{F}_{l^{\prime}}.

To prove that this indeed defines a section, we need only to show that for any composable pair of morphisms
(x\xrightarrow{f}y\xrightarrow{g}z)
we have
\alpha_{f}\circ f^{-1}\alpha_{g}\cong\alpha_{g\circ f}
. In other words, we need to prove the following isomorphism

\underset{(g^{*}w\rightarrow t)\in g^{*}/t}{\mathrm{colim}}\underset{(f^{*}t\rightarrow y)\in f^{*}/y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x,f^{*}g^{*}w)\cong\underset{(f^{*}g^{*}w\rightarrow y)\in f^{*}g^{*}/y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x,f^{*}g^{*}w),

which follows from
3.7
. It follows from the construction that this system of correspondences on various
\overline{p}^{-1}(g)
is compatible, i.e. it defines an element of

\underset{g:[m]\rightarrow\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\lim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\overline{p}^{-1}(g),*)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}((\mathpzc{Cat}^{(\mathrm{act},\rightarrow)}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U},*).

This defines a morphism

G:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n],\mathrm{Corr})^{\sim}\rightarrow(\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\sim}.

Observe that
H\circ G(F)\cong F
for any
F\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n],\mathrm{Corr})
. Indeed, it follows easily from the construction that the restrictions of
F
and
H\circ G(F)
to every
(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{k}
are isomorphic, which means that
F
and
H\circ G(F)
agree on objects and morphisms of
L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n]
meaning that they are isomorphic as functors. To prove that
G\circ H\cong\mathrm{id}
, first observe that
\mathscr{F}
and
G\circ H(\mathscr{F})
by definition agree on
\coprod_{m\geq 0}\overline{p}^{-1}([m])
, so it suffices to prove that they agree on
\overline{p}^{-1}(f)
for all morphisms
f:[m]\rightarrow[l]
in
\Delta
. To prove this observe that for a category
\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
with a presheaf
\mathscr{E}
the restriction of
\mathscr{E}
to
\mathscr{C}_{f}
for a morphism
f
in
\mathscr{D}
is given by

\underset{(y\rightarrow x)\in\mathscr{C}_{f}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{E}(y)\rightarrow\underset{(y\rightarrow x)\in\mathscr{C}_{/x}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{E}(y)\cong\mathscr{E}(x).

Applying it to our case, we see that the required morphism

f^{-1}G\circ H(\mathscr{F})_{[l]}\rightarrow G\circ H(\mathscr{F})_{[m]}

is given by

\displaystyle f^{-1}G\circ H(\mathscr{F})(x,y)\cong

\displaystyle\underset{f^{*}t\rightarrow y}{\mathrm{colim}}f^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}G\circ H(\mathscr{F})(x,t)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{f^{*}t\rightarrow y}{\mathrm{colim}}f^{*}_{\mathrm{Cell}}\mathscr{F}(x,t)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{f^{*}t\rightarrow y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x,f^{*}t)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\underset{w\rightarrow y}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x,w)\cong\mathscr{F}(x,y),

where the second isomorphisms follows from the observation that
\mathscr{F}
and
G\circ H(\mathscr{F})
coincide on
(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])_{l}
. This is exactly the same as the corresponding morphism for
\mathscr{F}
, which proves the claim.

To prove the isomorphism on morphisms, observe that it follows from the definition of
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},!}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([n],\mathrm{Corr})
and
4.42
that a morphism between two functors
\mathscr{F},\mathscr{G}:[n]\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
is given by the data of a natural transformation

\alpha_{i}:(L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n])\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1],\mathrm{Corr}_{i})

between functors
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
for which the compositions
s\circ\alpha_{i}
(resp
t\circ\alpha_{i}
) with the source morphism
s:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1],\mathrm{Corr}_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{i}
(resp. the target morphism
t:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([1],\mathrm{Corr}_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}_{i}
) are isomorphic to the lax functor
\mathscr{F}
(resp.
\mathscr{G}
). The construction considered above then easily identifies this data with a morphism between the corresponding objects
G(\mathscr{F})
and
G(\mathscr{G})
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
.
∎

Corollary 5.19
.

Given a Segal space
\mathscr{C}
there is an equivalence between the category of monads on
\mathscr{C}
in
\mathrm{Corr}
and the category
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{\mathscr{C}/}
of morphisms
f:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
that induce an isomorphism on the groupoid of objects.

Proof.

This follows from
theorem

5.18
and
4.31
.
∎

Corollary 5.20
.

For a Segal space
\mathscr{C}
there is an equivalence

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}^{\mathrm{lax},!}(\mathscr{C},\mathrm{Span})\cong\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}.

Proof.

Recall from
2.17
that
\mathrm{Span}
can be viewed as a full subcategory of
\mathrm{Corr}
on constant Segal spaces. The isomorphism of
theorem

5.18
then identifies
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}^{\mathrm{lax},!}(\mathscr{C},\mathrm{Span})
with the subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
on the objects of the form
(X\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{D})
where
X
is a constant simplicial space. Since
f
is supposed to be an isomorphism on objects, it follows that
X\cong\mathscr{D}_{0}
, and so it follows that this subcategory is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
.
∎

Corollary 5.21
.

For a Segal space
\mathscr{C}
there is an equivalence

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}^{\mathrm{lax},\mathrm{unit},!}(\mathscr{C},\mathrm{Corr})\cong\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}.

Proof.

First of all, observe that by
4.34
and
2.2
it suffices to prove the claim for
\mathscr{C}\cong[n]
. Now observe that the unital lax functors
[n]\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
can be identified with those morphisms
L^{\mathrm{lax}}[n]\rightarrow\mathrm{Corr}
that send objects of the form
([k]\overset{a}{\twoheadrightarrow}[l]\xrightarrow{\{i\}}[n])
where
k>0
and
\{i\}
denotes the constant morphism with image
i\in[n]
to strings of identity correspondences. Untangling the constructions of
theorem

5.18
and preceding propositions, we see that those functors correspond to
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
for which
\mathscr{F}(\langle m\rangle\xrightarrow{f}[n])\cong\mathscr{F}([m]\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}[n])
, where
\widetilde{f}
has the same underlying object in
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
except all the edges are marked with
0
. Observe that there is a natural inclusion
i:\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
sending each
[l]\xrightarrow{g}[n]
to the corresponding object of
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
in which all edges are marked with
0
. This morphism induces a fully faithful inclusion
i_{!}:\mathrm{Cat}_{/[n]}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
, and objects
\mathscr{F}
as above are easily seen to be precisely the objects in the image of
i_{!}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Notation 5.22
.

For a given Segal space
\mathscr{C}
we denote by
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
on the morphisms over
\mathscr{C}
of the form
(\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{D})
where
\mathscr{E}
is complete and by
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{comp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the full subcategory on the objects as above for which both
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{D}
are complete.

Proposition 5.23
.

The natural inclusion
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

Indeed, the left adjoint in question is easily seen to be the functor sending
\mathscr{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
to
\mathscr{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}\xrightarrow{\widehat{(-)}}\mathrm{Corr}
, where
\widehat{(-)}
is the functor of
3.17
.
∎

Remark 5.24
.

It follows from
remark

2.11
that, given a monad
T
in
\mathrm{Cat}
over a Segal space
\mathscr{C}
, we have an induced monad
T_{!}
in
\mathrm{Corr}
. Applying to it the construction of
theorem

5.18
we obtain a ”naive” version of the Kleisli category
\mathscr{K}(T)
. More specifically, it is a Segal space with the same space of objects as
\mathscr{C}
whose morphisms are given by

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{K}(T)}(x,y)\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,Ty)

with composition given by
f\xrightarrow{f}Ty\xrightarrow{Tg}T^{2}z\xrightarrow{m}Tz
. The problem with this construction is that, even if
\mathscr{C}
is a complete Segal space,
\mathscr{K}(T)
generally is not.

Proposition 5.25
.

Given a complete Segal space
\mathscr{C}
there is an equivalence between
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
and the category
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
whose objects are morphisms
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E}
over
\mathscr{C}
that induce an effective epimorphism on the space of objects and such that for every non-unital morphism
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{C}
we have an isomorphism

(\mathscr{D}_{f})^{\sim}\cong\mathscr{D}_{0,x}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0,x}}(\mathscr{E}_{f})^{\sim}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0,y}}\mathscr{D}_{0,y}.

Proof.

Given an object
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{g}\mathscr{K}
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
observe that since
\mathscr{C}
is complete, the morphism
\mathscr{K}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
factors through
\widehat{\mathscr{K}}
. Observe that the composite
\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{K}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{K}}
is essentially surjective on objects and moreover we have

\mathscr{D}_{0,x}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,x}}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}^{\sim}_{f}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,y}}\mathscr{D}_{0,y}\cong\mathscr{D}_{0,x}\times_{\mathscr{K}_{0,x}}\mathscr{K}^{\sim}_{f}\times_{\mathscr{K}_{0,y}}\mathscr{D}_{0,y}\cong\mathscr{D}^{\sim}_{f},

where we have used the isomorphism

\mathscr{K}_{0,x}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,x}}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}^{\sim}_{f}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,x}}\mathscr{K}_{0,x}\cong\mathscr{K}^{\sim}_{f}

of
3.15
and the isomorphisms
\mathscr{D}_{0,x}\cong\mathscr{K}_{0,x}
and
\mathscr{D}_{0,y}\cong\mathscr{K}_{0,y}
which follow from the definition of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
. It follows that
\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{K}}
is an object of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, and so we have defined a functor
F:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
. Conversely, given an object
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E}
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
we will define a functor
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
by sending every
0
-marked edge
a:[1]\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
to
\mathscr{D}^{\sim}_{f}
and every
1
-marked edge
b:\langle 1\rangle\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
to
\mathscr{D}_{0,s(b)}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0,s(b)}}\mathscr{E}^{\sim}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0,t(b)}}\mathscr{D}_{0,t(b)}
with the obvious operation of composition. This defines a functor
G:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
.

The fact that
G\circ F\cong\mathrm{id}
once again follows from the isomorphism

\mathscr{K}_{0,x}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,x}}\widehat{\mathscr{K}}^{\sim}_{f}\times_{\widehat{\mathscr{K}}_{0,x}}\mathscr{K}_{0,x}\cong\mathscr{K}^{\sim}_{f}

of
3.15
. It remains to prove that
F\circ G\cong\mathrm{id}
. So assume we are given an object
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E}
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, denote by
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{\overline{f}}\overline{\mathscr{E}}
the objects
G(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})
. First, observe that the space of morphisms of
\overline{\mathscr{E}}
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}
. In particular, since
\mathscr{E}
is complete we see that the subcategory of invertible morphisms
(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}
has
\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}
as the space of morphisms. By examining the constructions of
[
AF18
]
, we see that

\widehat{\overline{\mathscr{E}}}_{0}\cong\underset{[n]\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{n}\cong\underset{[n]\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\overbrace{\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}...\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}}^{\text{n times}}\cong\mathscr{E}_{0},

where the last isomorphism follows since
\mathscr{D}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{0}
is an epimorphism. Similarly we see that for a morphism
b:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{C}
we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\overline{\mathscr{E}}}_{b}\cong

\displaystyle\underset{([n],[m])\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{n}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}}\overline{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{m}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{([n],[m])\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{n}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{m}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{([n],[m])\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\times\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{n}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}(\overline{\mathscr{E}})^{\sim}_{m}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{b}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{0}\cong\mathscr{E}_{b}

which concludes the proof that
F\circ G\cong\mathrm{id}
.
∎

Theorem 5.26
.

Given an object
(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, the category
\mathscr{E}
can be characterized as the lax colimit of the corresponding lax functor
F:\mathscr{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
.

Proof.

First, observe that since

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{K})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\mathscr{E}\times\mathscr{K}^{\mathrm{op}},*)

by
3.3
, it suffices to prove that

\mathrm{coCone}_{\mathrm{Corr}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(F,*)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\mathscr{E},*).

For
g:[n]\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
denote by
\mathscr{E}_{g}
the pullback
\mathscr{E}\times_{\mathscr{C}}[n]
, then we have

\mathscr{E}\cong\underset{g:[n]\rightarrow\mathscr{C}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{E}_{g}.

Combining this with
4.38
, we see that it suffices to prove the proposition for
\mathscr{C}\cong[n]
.

We first prove the claim under the assumption that the functor
F:[n]\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
actually lands in
\mathrm{Span}
. Then it follows from the definition of the lax cocone together with
2.17
and the fact that
*\in\mathrm{Span}
that
\mathrm{coCone}_{\mathrm{Corr}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(F,*)\cong\mathrm{coCone}_{\mathrm{Span}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(F,*)
. It now follows from
5.20
that giving a lax cocone under
F
is equivalent to giving an object
\mathscr{F}\in\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n+1]}
such that its restriction to
[0,n]\subset[n+1]
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{E}\in\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
and such that
\mathscr{F}_{n+1}\cong*
.

Denote by
\widetilde{j}:\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n+1]}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n+1]}
the subcategory having the same objects and such that for
h:[m]\rightarrow[n]
and
g:[l]\rightarrow[n]
we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n]}}(h,g)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}}(h,g)

if
h(m)<n+1
and if
h(m)=n+1
denote by
k_{g}
the object of
[l]
for which
g(k_{g})=j<n+1
but
g(k_{g}+1)=n+1
if such an object exists (and introduce similar notation for
h
), then
\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n]}}(h,g)
is given by those morphisms
g\rightarrow h
over
[n]
in
\Delta
for which
g(l)=n+1
and
g(k_{g})=h(k_{f})
. Observe that the category of functors
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n+1]}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
that satisfy the Segal condition and whose restriction to
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}\subset\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n+1]}
is given by
\mathscr{E}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\coprod_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i},*)
. Indeed, it is easy to see from the definition of
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n+1]}
that the functors
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
satisfying these conditions are determined by their values
\mathscr{F}_{i}
on
[1]\xrightarrow{[i,n+1]}[n+1]
an those
\mathscr{F}_{i}
have no additional structure except for morphisms
(\mathscr{E}_{i})_{n}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{i})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{i}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{i}
that assemble to give them the structure of an object of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\mathscr{E}_{i},*)
. It will follow from the calculation below that
\widetilde{j}
takes
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\coprod_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{i},*)
to
\mathrm{coCone}_{\mathrm{Span}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(F,*)
and that
\widetilde{j}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{!}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}\cong j^{*}j_{!}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
in the notation of
3.23
. Since
\widetilde{j}
is surjective on objects, it will then follow from
[
Lur
, Theorem 4.7.3.5.]
that
\mathrm{coCone}_{\mathrm{Span}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(F,*)
is isomorphic to the category of algebras for the monad
\widetilde{j}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{!}\cong j^{*}j_{!}
, which is in turn isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(\mathscr{E},*)
by the proof of
3.23
.

Finally, observe that

(\widetilde{j}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{!}\mathscr{F})_{i}\cong\underset{g\in\mathpzc{Cat}_{i,/[n]}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{E}_{i,g(1)}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{g(1)})_{0}}\mathscr{E}_{g(1),g(2)}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{g(1)})_{0}}...\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{g(m)})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{g(m)}

where
\mathpzc{Cat}_{i,/[n]}
is the category whose objects are morphisms
g:[m+1]\rightarrow[n+1]
for which
g(m+1)=n+1
and whose morphisms are morphisms
g\rightarrow f
in
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{/[n]}
. Denote by
\overline{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{i,/[n]}
the full subcategory of
\mathpzc{Cat}_{i,/[n]}
on those
g
for which
g(1)=g(k_{g})
, then
\overline{j}:\overline{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{i,/[n]}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}_{i,/[n]}
is a cofinal subcategory. Indeed, for every
g\in\mathpzc{Cat}_{i/[n]}
the category
g/\overline{j}
contains the final object given by the natural morphism
\overline{g}\rightarrow g
over
[n]
in
\Delta
, where
\overline{g}
is obtained from
g
by deleting all the points
k>0
of
[m+1]
such that
g(k)<g(k_{g})
. It follows that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{j}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{!}\mathscr{F})_{i}\cong

\displaystyle\underset{g\in\overline{\mathpzc{Cat}}_{i,/[n]}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{E}_{i,g(1)}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{g(1)}}\mathscr{E}_{g(1),g(2)}\times_{\mathscr{E}_{g(1)}}...\times_{\mathscr{E}_{g(m)}}\mathscr{F}_{g(m)}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\underset{(g:[1]\rightarrow[n],g(0)=i)}{\mathrm{colim}}\underset{l\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\;\mathscr{E}_{i,g(1)}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{g(1)})_{0}}(\mathscr{E}_{g(1)})_{l}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{g(1)})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{g(1)}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\coprod_{g:[1]\rightarrow[n],g(0)=i}\mathscr{F}_{g(1)}\circ\mathscr{E}_{g(0),g(1)},

where in the last line we view
\mathscr{E}_{g(0),g(1)}
as a correspondence and
\circ
denotes the composition of correspondences. It now follows from the proof of
3.23
that
\widetilde{j}^{*}\widetilde{j}_{!}\mathscr{F}\cong j^{*}j_{!}\mathscr{F}
which concludes the proof in this special case.

Now assume that
(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})
is a general object of
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
. Just like in the special case treated above, it follows from
theorem

5.18
that giving an object of
\mathrm{coCone}^{\mathrm{lax}}_{\mathrm{Corr}}(F,*)
is equivalent to providing an object of
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
of the form
((\mathscr{D},*)\xrightarrow{(f,\mathrm{id})}(\mathscr{E},*))
. We will now show that this object is uniquely determined by its restriction
(\mathscr{E},*)
to
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
. Assume that we are given such an object, so in particular we are given the spaces
\mathscr{F}_{i}
lying over
[i,n+1]
together with compatible system of actions
\mathscr{E}_{i,j}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{j})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{j}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}_{i}
. We will prove that this uniquely determines the action of
\mathscr{D}_{i,j}
on
\mathscr{F}_{j}
for all
i
and
j
, which would conclude the proof. Indeed, it follows from the definition of
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
that we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{i,j}\times_{(\mathscr{E}_{j})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}_{i,j}\times_{(\mathscr{D}_{j})_{0}}\mathscr{F}_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in which the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism, which obviously proves our claim and concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.27
.

Since
\mathrm{Corr}\cong\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{op}}
, the category
\mathscr{E}
is also a lax limit of the corresponding functor.

Lemma 5.28
.

Fix a Segal space
\mathscr{C}
together with an object
(\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E})
of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
such that the image of all morphisms of
\mathscr{D}
is invertible in
\mathscr{E}
. Then the left Kan extension
p_{!}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U},\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}},\mathscr{S})
(where
U
is the functor of
5.11
) takes
(\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E})
to an object of
\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, where we view
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
as a full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}((\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U},\mathscr{S})
using
remark

5.13
.

Proof.

First, observe that it is enough to prove the lemma for
\mathscr{C}\cong[n]
. Indeed, it follows from
5.9
and
2.2
that a functor
\mathscr{F}:\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
(resp.
\mathscr{E}:\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
) satisfies the respective Segal condition if and only if this is true for all
v^{*}\mathscr{F}
(resp.
v^{*}\mathscr{E}
) for
v\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([n],\mathscr{C})
and
n\geq 0
. The claim now follows from the Beck Chevalley isomorphism for the pullback square

\hbox to162.47pt{\vbox to74.43pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 81.23625pt\lower-37.21233pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.0222pt}{-23.7832pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The value of the left Kan extension on a given
w:[m]\rightarrow[n]
is given by

p_{!}\mathscr{F}(w)\cong\underset{(p(x)\rightarrow w)\in(p/w)}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{F}(x).

Observe that there is a natural inclusion
i:p^{-1}(w)\hookrightarrow(p/w)
, we will prove that it is cofinal. To do this, first recall from
5.12
that the fiber of
p
over
w
is given by
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{/[m]}
. It follows that the objects of
(p/w)
are given by pairs
y\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}(f:[m]\rightarrow[l],h:[k]\rightarrow[l])
where
f
is a morphism over
[n]
and
h
is a cellular morphism. The objects of
(y/i)
are then given by pairs
(g:[k]\rightarrow[m],\alpha:f\circ g\cong h)
. Observe that we then have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(y/i)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{h\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{l}/f/g}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\mathrm{Cell}}_{l}/f\circ g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

in the notation of
2.9
. Now observe that since
h
and
g
are assumed to be cellular, the vertical maps are homotopy equivalences. The bottom horizontal map is cofinal by the proof of
2.9
, so in particular it is a homotopy equivalence. It follows that the top horizontal map is also a homotopy equivalence, which means exactly that
(y/i)
is contractible, so
i
is cofinal.

Now observe that the natural inclusion
i:\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}\hookrightarrow(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{int}}_{/U}
is also cofinal, so it suffices to take colimit over the fiber of
U
. Finally, observe that this fiber is isomorphic to
(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{m}
, in particular it follows that

U_{w}\cong\underset{(w\rightarrowtail e)\in(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el}}_{w/}}{\lim}U_{e}.

Moreover, the Segal condition for
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
implies that

\mathscr{F}(x_{w})\cong\underset{(w\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el}}_{w/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(i_{!}x),

where
x_{w}
is an element of
U_{w}
and
\{i_{!}x\}
are its projections to various
U_{e}
under the isomorphism above. The only thing that remains to be proved is the fact that
U_{w}
-colimits distribute over
(\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el}}_{w/}
-limits. We first make the condition more explicit. Denote by
\mathscr{E}^{i}_{m}
(resp. by
\mathscr{D}_{m}^{i}
) for
i\in\{0,1,...,n\}
the value of this space the constant morphism
\langle m\rangle\rightarrow[n]
(resp.
[m]\rightarrow[n]
) with value
i
and by
\mathscr{E}_{1}^{i,j}
the value on
\langle 1\rangle\rightarrow[n]
that sends
0
to
i
and
1
to
j
. In these notations we need to prove the following isomorphism

(7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\underset{\{m_{i}\}_{i\in\{0,1,...,n\}}\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}})\times_{\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}}...\times_{\mathscr{D}^{n-1}_{m_{n-1}}}(\mathscr{D}^{n-1}_{m_{n-1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{n-1}}\mathscr{E}^{n-1,n}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{n}}\mathscr{D}^{n}_{m_{n}})\\
\cong&\underset{(m_{0},m_{1})\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{2}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}})\times_{\underset{m_{1}\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}}...\times_{\underset{m_{n-1}\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\mathscr{D}^{n-1}_{m_{n-1}}}\underset{(m_{n-1},m_{n})\in(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{2}}{\mathrm{colim}}(\mathscr{D}^{n-1}_{m_{n-1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{n-1}}\mathscr{E}^{n-1,n}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{n}}\mathscr{D}^{n}_{m_{n}}).\end{split}

We first assume that
n=2
. Denote by

p_{0,1}:\mathscr{D}^{0,1}_{m_{0,1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0,1}}\mathscr{E}^{(0,1),(1,2)}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1,2}}\mathscr{D}^{1,2}_{m_{1,2}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}

and by

p_{2}:\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{E}^{1,2}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{2}}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{m_{2}}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}

the natural projections, where we view
p_{0,1}
(resp.
p_{2}
) as morphisms of the corresponding right fibrations over
(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{2}
and
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
(resp. over
(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{3}
and
(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}})^{2}
). With these notations we need to prove that the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{E}^{1,2}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{2}}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{m_{2}}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathscr{D}^{0}_{m_{0}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{0}}\mathscr{E}^{0,1}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{1}}\mathscr{E}^{1,2}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{D}_{0}^{2}}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{m_{2}}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}|}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|p_{2}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|p_{0}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|p_{1}|}

is a pullback square, where
|-|
denotes the functor of geometric realization of an
\infty
-category. To prove it we will make use of Quillen’s theorem B in the form it is presented in
[
AF17
, Theorem 5.16.]
. Under suitable conditions it allows us to identify the fiber of
|p_{0}|
over
\overline{\alpha}
in
|\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}|
with
|p_{0}/\overline{\alpha}|
. Observe that, since
p_{0}
is a coCartesian fibration, we have an isomorphism

(8)

\displaystyle|p_{0}/\overline{\alpha}|\cong|p_{0}^{-1}(\overline{\alpha})|

and that similar statement holds for
p_{2}
. To prove that the commutative square above is a pullback it suffices to prove that
|p_{2}|^{-1}(\overline{\beta})\cong|p_{0}|^{-1}(|p_{1}|(\overline{\beta}))
, however this easily follows from
eq.

8
and Quillen’s theorem B since
p_{2}
is a pullback of
p_{0}
.

It now remains to prove that the conditions of Quillen’s theorem B are satisfied for
p_{0}
. More explicitly, we need to prove that for any morphism
a:\overline{\alpha}\rightarrow\overline{\alpha}^{\prime}
the induced functor
|p_{0}^{-1}(\overline{\alpha})|\rightarrow|p_{0}^{-1}(\overline{\alpha}^{\prime})|
is an isomorphism. An object
\overline{\alpha}
of
\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}
can be identified with a string of morphisms

(d_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{2}}...\xrightarrow{\alpha_{m}}d_{m})

in
\mathscr{D}^{1}
, the objects of the fiber
p_{0}^{-1}(\overline{\alpha})
are given by strings

(9)

\displaystyle(d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\beta_{1}}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\beta_{2}}...\xrightarrow{\beta_{l}}d^{\prime}_{l}\xrightarrow{c}d_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{2}}...\xrightarrow{\alpha_{m}}d_{m}),

where
c
is a morphism in
\mathscr{E}^{0,1}
and
d^{\prime}_{i}
are objects of
\mathscr{D}^{0}
. Observe that any morphism in
\mathscr{D}^{1}_{m_{1}}
lying over an active morphism in
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
acts as an identity on the fibers of
p_{0}
, the same is true for inert morphisms that preserve the minimal element. For an inert morphism
[k]\rightarrowtail[m]
in
\Delta
that sends
0
to
s
the corresponding action on the fiber sends the string (
9
) above to

(d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\beta_{1}}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\beta_{2}}...\xrightarrow{\beta_{l}}d^{\prime}_{l}\xrightarrow{f(\alpha_{s})\circ f(\alpha_{s-1})\circ...\circ f(\alpha_{1})\circ c}d_{s}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s+1}}d_{s+1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{s+2}}...\xrightarrow{\alpha_{m}}d_{m}).

However, since the images under
f
of all morphisms in
\mathscr{D}
were assumed to be invertible in
\mathscr{E}
, we see that this morphism also induces an isomorphism on fibers. Thus we have proved that the conditions of Quillen’s theorem B apply, which suffices to prove the statement for
n=2
by our previous considerations. Finally, the claim for
n>2
follows by iterated application of the same argument.
∎

Remark 5.29
.

Given an object
(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
satisfying the conditions of
lemma

5.28
, the category
p_{!}(\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E})
over
\mathscr{C}
is obtained by taking the images under
f
of the morphisms in the fibers
\mathscr{D}_{x}
and contracting them.

Construction 5.30
.

For a given Segal space
\mathscr{C}
denote by
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the category whose objects are morphisms
f:\langle n\rangle\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
, where
\langle n\rangle
is an interval of length
n
with edges marked with
0
or
1
and
f
sends all edges marked with
0
to identities, and morphisms are morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
that preserve the markings. We will call a morphism active (resp. inert) if the underlying morphism in
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
is active (resp. inert) and we will call an object elementary if the underlying object of
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
is elementary, this gives
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the structure of an algebraic pattern. Observe that there is a natural faithful inclusion

i:\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}.

Remark 5.31
.

The Segal spaces for
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
are easily seen to be given by a pair of morphisms
(\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\sim})
in
\mathrm{Cat}
whose spaces of objects are isomorphic.

Lemma 5.32
.

The left Kan extension along
i
induces a functor between the categories of respective Segal spaces.

Proof.

First, observe that we can reduce to the case
\mathscr{C}\cong[n]
using the same argument as in
lemma

5.28
. For a given
x\in\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
denote by
(i/x)^{\mathrm{act}}\hookrightarrow(i/x)
the full subcategory of
(i/x)
on active morphisms
i(y)\twoheadrightarrow x
(for the duration of the proof we will use the words active morphism and inert morphism to refer to morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
whose underlying morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
are active or inert, this is
different
to active and inert morphisms in
\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
itself). The active/inert factorization in
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
shows that
(i/x)^{\mathrm{act}}
is a cofinal subcategory. Observe that although
i
does not have unique lifting of inert morphism, it does have unique lifting for those morphisms that preserve the markings. In particular, it has unique lifting for all morphisms in
(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el},\mathrm{mc}}_{x/}
, which is a coinitial subcategory in
(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el}}_{x/}
. It follows from this and the properties of
\mathpzc{Cat}_{/[n]}
that we have an isomorphism

(i/x)^{\mathrm{act}}\cong\underset{(x\rightarrowtail e)\in(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el},\mathrm{mc}}_{x/}}{\lim}(i/e)^{\mathrm{act}}.

Moreover, the Segal condition for
\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}
implies that for a Segal space
\mathscr{F}\in\mathrm{Seg}_{\widetilde{\mathpzc{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]}}(\mathscr{S})
and an active morphism
i(y)\twoheadrightarrow x
we have

\mathscr{F}(y)\cong\underset{(x\overset{i}{\rightarrowtail}e)\in(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el},\mathrm{mc}}_{x/}}{\lim}\mathscr{F}(i_{!}y).

So it remains to prove that
(i/x)^{\mathrm{act}}
-colimits distribute over
(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el},\mathrm{mc}}_{x/}
-limits. To do this, first observe that
(i/\{j\})^{\mathrm{act}}\cong*
where
\{j\}
denotes any morphism of the form
([0]\xrightarrow{\{j\}}[n])
. It follows from this and the observations above that

(i/x)^{\mathrm{act}}\cong\underset{(x\rightarrowtail e)\in(\mathpzc{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/[n]})^{\mathrm{el},\mathrm{mc}}_{x/}}{\lim}(i/e)^{\mathrm{act}}\cong\prod_{x\rightarrowtail e,e\in\{[1],\langle 1\rangle\}}(i/e)^{\mathrm{act}}.

Now the claim follows just as at the end of the proof of
2.4
.
∎

Remark 5.33
.

Given an object
(\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\sim})
, the category
i_{!}(\mathscr{D},\mathscr{E})
has the same space of objects as
\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{E}
and its morphisms are given by strings
(x_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}x_{2}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}...\xrightarrow{f_{n}}x_{n+1})
where each
f_{i}
is a morphism either in
\mathscr{D}
or in
\mathscr{E}
modulo the equivalence relation that identifies

(y_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}...\xrightarrow{g_{i}}y_{i+1}\xrightarrow{g_{i+1}}y_{i+2}\xrightarrow{g_{i+2}}y_{i+3}\xrightarrow{g_{i+3}}...\xrightarrow{g_{n}}y_{n+1})

with

(y_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}...\xrightarrow{g_{i}}y_{i+1}\xrightarrow{g_{i+2}\circ g_{i+1}}y_{i+3}\xrightarrow{g_{i+3}}...\xrightarrow{g_{n}}y_{n+1})

if both
g_{i+1}
and
g_{i+2}
belong to the same category.

Proposition 5.34
.

Denote by
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{eso}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
on those morphisms
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{C}
that induce an epimorphism on objects and by
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
the full subcategory on morphisms that induce an isomorphism o objects. Then the natural inclusion
i:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{eso}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
admits a left adjoint
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}
. Moreover, if
\mathscr{D}
is a complete Segal space, then so is
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}
.

Proof.

Given a morphism
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{C}
first consider the object
(\mathscr{D},C_{\bullet}(f_{0}))
where
C_{\bullet}(f_{0})
denotes the Cech nerve of
f_{0}:\mathscr{D}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{0}
, i.e. it is a groupoid whose space of objects is given by
\mathscr{D}_{0}
and morphisms are
\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}
. We then define

L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}p_{!}i_{!}(\mathscr{D},C_{\bullet}(f_{0})).

First, observe that
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}
indeed belongs to
\mathrm{Cat}_{/\mathscr{C}}
since by
remark

5.33
the images of morphisms in
C_{\bullet}(f_{0})
in
i_{!}(\mathscr{D},C_{\bullet}(f_{0}))
are invertible, and so this follows from
lemma

5.28
. Moreover, it even lies in
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
since

L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}_{0}\cong\underset{\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}{\mathrm{colim}}\overbrace{\mathscr{D}_{0}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{0}}...\times_{\mathscr{C}_{0}}\mathscr{D}_{0}}^{\text{$n$ times}}\cong\mathscr{C}_{0}

because
f
was assumed to be an effective epimorphism. To prove the required universal property observe that for any
\mathscr{E}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{i.o.}}_{/\mathscr{C}}}(L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D},\mathscr{E})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}}((\mathscr{D},C_{\bullet}(f_{0})),i^{*}p^{*}\mathscr{E})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Cat}}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}}((\mathscr{D},C_{\bullet}(f_{0})),(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}_{0})).

The objects of the latter space are given by pairs of morphisms
(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{g}\mathscr{E},C_{\bullet}(f_{0})\xrightarrow{h}\mathscr{E}_{0})
over
\mathscr{C}
. Observe that by definition
\mathscr{E}_{0}\cong\mathscr{C}_{0}
, so there is a unique morphism
h:C_{\bullet}(f_{0})\rightarrow\mathscr{E}_{0}
which proves the first claim.

To prove the second claim observe using
remark

5.29
and
remark

5.33
that the morphisms in
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}
are given by strings
(d_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}d_{2}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}...\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}d_{n})
of not necessarily composable morphisms in
\mathscr{D}
such that their images in
\mathscr{C}
form a composable string of morphisms modulo the equivalence relation identifying

(d_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}d_{2}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}...\xrightarrow{f_{i}}d_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f_{i+1}}d_{i+2}\xrightarrow{f_{i+2}}d_{i+3}\xrightarrow{f_{i+3}}...\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}d_{n})

with

(d_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}d_{2}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}...\xrightarrow{f_{i}}d_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f_{i+2}\circ f_{i+1}}d_{i+3}\xrightarrow{f_{i+3}}...\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}d_{n})

if
f_{i+1}
and
f_{i+2}
are actually composable in
\mathscr{D}
. In particular, it follows easily that invertible morphisms in
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{D}
are given by strings
(d\xrightarrow{f}e)
where
f
is an invertible morphism in
\mathscr{D}
, and those are all identities by assumption.
∎

Corollary 5.35
.

The natural inclusion
i:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{comp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
admits a left adjoint
L^{\mathrm{comp}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathscr{E}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{D})
be an element of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, so in particular
\mathscr{E}
is a complete Segal space. Let
(\mathscr{K}\xrightarrow{g}\mathscr{L})
be some element of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{comp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
, then we see that for every morphism
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})\xrightarrow{(u,v)}(\mathscr{K},\mathscr{L})
the morphism
v
factors as
\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{p}\widehat{\mathscr{D}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{v}}\mathscr{L}
since
\mathscr{L}
is complete. We will define
L^{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
to be given by
(\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}\widehat{\mathscr{D}})
, where

\widetilde{\mathscr{E}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}(p\circ f)

in the notation of
5.34
. Then we have the following commutative diagram over
\mathscr{C}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

We need to prove that the space of dotted arrows making the diagram commute is contractible. Observe that from the definition of
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}
it follows that this space can be described as the space of morphisms

C_{\bullet}(p_{0}\circ f_{0})\xrightarrow{h}\mathscr{K}_{0}

such that
g_{0}\circ h\cong v_{0}\circ f_{0}
. Observe that this space is contractible since
g_{0}
is an isomorphism by the definition of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
.
∎

6.
Completeness for monads and theories

In this short final section we reap the fruits of our labours and prove the main results of this paper -
theorem

6.4
and
theorem

6.9
. The former of these provides an isomorphism between the category of
\mathscr{C}
-theories for a given category
\mathscr{C}
and the category of lax functors from
\mathscr{C}
to the flagged bicategory of correspondences with arities. This specializes to the main result of
[
BMW12
]
in the case
\mathscr{C}\cong*
.

The latter has to do with the notion of completeness. This notion is unique to higher category theory and was first introduced in a closely related context in
[
CH19
]
. More specifically, we introduce the notion of complete
\mathscr{C}
-theory in
6.2
and
theorem

6.9
states that for a certain class of theories (which we call good) described in
6.7
we can introduce the functor of completion that is left adjoint to the inclusion of the category of complete theories into the category of good theories. In particular, it specializes to the completion functor of
[
CH19
]
for the theories associated to algebraic patterns.

Definition 6.1
.

Denote by
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}
the following twofold Segal space: its objects are given by complete Segal spaces
\mathscr{C}
equipped with a full subcategory
i_{\mathscr{C}}:\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{S})
. We define the category of morphisms from
\mathscr{C}
to
\mathscr{D}
to be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
on those correspondences
K:\mathscr{C}\nrightarrow\mathscr{D}
that take
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{C}}
to
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{D}}
(under the equivalence
\mathrm{Corr}(*,\mathscr{C})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{S})
). We will call the objects of
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}
the
categories with arities
and we will say that the correspondences in
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}

respect arities
. For a complete Segal space
\mathscr{K}
we will denote by
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},\mathrm{art}}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{K},\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}})
the category whose objects are the lax functors
F:\mathscr{K}\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}
and whose morphisms are natural transformations in
\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},!}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{K},\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}})
for which we in addition require that any component
\alpha_{c,!}:F(k)\rightarrow G(k)
for
c\in\mathscr{C}
respects the arities.

Definition 6.2
.

For a complete Segal space
\mathscr{C}
denote by
\mathrm{Th}(\mathscr{C})
the category whose objects
\mathscr{T}
are given by morphisms
(\mathscr{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{t}\mathscr{T}_{1})
over
\mathscr{C}
that belong to
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
such that in addition every
\mathscr{T}_{0,c}
is a category with arities
\mathscr{E}_{c}
, for every non-unital morphism
f:c\rightarrow c^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{C}
the correspondence
\mathscr{T}_{0,f}:\mathscr{T}_{0,c}\nrightarrow\mathscr{T}_{0,c^{\prime}}
respects arities and for every
c
the correspondence
t_{c}^{*}t_{c,!}:\mathscr{T}_{0,c}\nrightarrow\mathscr{T}_{0,c}
also respects arities. We will refer to the objects of
\mathrm{Th}(\mathscr{C})
as
\mathscr{C}
-theories
. We will call a
\mathscr{C}
-theory
\mathscr{T}

complete
if the underlying object of
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{lcomp},\overset{\mathrm{eso}}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
belongs to
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{comp},{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
and denote the corresponding subcategory by
\mathrm{Th}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathscr{C})
. We will call a functor
\mathscr{F}:\mathscr{T}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
a
model
for the theory if for every
c\in\mathscr{C}
its restriction to
\mathscr{T}_{0,c}
belongs to
\mathscr{E}_{c}
; we will denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{T}_{1},\mathscr{S})
on models by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{S})
.

Remark 6.3
.

For the theories
\mathscr{T}
of the type described in
6.6
one can more generally define for any presentable category
\mathscr{V}
a subcategory
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{V}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{D},\mathscr{V})\overset{i_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C}_{0},\mathscr{V})
and further define the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{V})
of
\mathscr{V}
-models of
\mathscr{T}
to be the category of functors
\mathscr{C}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{V}
whose restriction to
\mathscr{C}_{0}
belongs to
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{V}}
, however we will not need this construction.

Theorem 6.4
.

For every complete Segal space
\mathscr{C}
there is an equivalence

\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{lax},\mathrm{art}}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C},\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}})\cong\mathrm{Th}(\mathscr{C}).

Moreover, under this equivalence the category of models for a theory
\mathscr{T}
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Cone}_{\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}}^{\mathrm{lax}}(*,F_{\mathscr{T}})
for the corresponding lax functor
F_{\mathscr{T}}
, where
*
denotes the terminal category viewed as a category with arities by declaring
\mathscr{E}_{*}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathscr{S}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows immediately from
theorem

5.18
after an elementary observation that for every functor
F:\mathscr{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{Corr}
with the corresponding object
(\mathscr{D}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{E})\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
and for every object
c\in\mathscr{C}
the monad corresponding to
*\xrightarrow{\{c\}}\mathscr{C}\overset{F}{\rightsquigarrow}\mathrm{Corr}
is given by
f_{c}^{*}f_{c,!}
.

To prove the the second claim, first observe that to every lax cone from
*
to
F_{\mathscr{T}}
we can attach a functor
\mathscr{F}:\mathscr{T}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
using
theorem

5.26
. Observe that it follows directly from the definitions that this cone lies in
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}
if and only if the restrictions
\mathscr{F}|_{\mathscr{T}_{0,c}}
belong to
\mathscr{E}_{c}
for all
c\in\mathscr{C}
, which is exactly the definition of a model for
\mathscr{T}
.
∎

Corollary 6.5
.

For a complete Segal space
\mathscr{D}
with arities
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{D}}
the category of monads in
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{art}}
with the underlying object
\mathscr{D}
is equivalent to the category of functors
F:\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}
that are epimorphisms on objects and such that
F^{*}F_{!}\mathscr{E}\subset\mathscr{E}
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
5.19
and
theorem

6.4
.
∎

Example 6.6
.

A category with arities can be associated to the data of a complete Segal space
\mathscr{C}_{0}
, an isomorphism-on-objects functor
\mathscr{C}_{0}\xrightarrow{f}\mathscr{C}_{1}
and a full subcategory
i:\mathscr{D}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{0}
. In this case
\mathscr{E}_{\mathscr{C}}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{D},\mathscr{S})\overset{i_{*}}{\hookrightarrow}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{C}_{0},\mathscr{S})
. The construction of
[
CH19
, Section 13]
allows us to attach a theory to every cartesian monad
T
on a presheaf category, namely the pair
(\mathscr{U}(T)\xrightarrow{j}\mathscr{W}(T))
with the arities given by
\mathscr{I}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{U}(T)
in the notation of
[
CH19
]
.

Definition 6.7
.

Given a
\mathscr{C}
theory
\mathscr{T}
denote by
u
the natural morphism
(\mathscr{T}_{0}\rightarrow L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{T}_{0})
. We will cal the theory
good
if it satisfies the following list of conditions:

•

For every object
c\in\mathscr{C}
we have
u_{c,!}u^{*}_{c}\cong\mathrm{id}
.

•

For every non-unital morphism
f:c_{1}\rightarrow c_{2}
we have
\mathscr{T}_{0,f}\cong u^{*}_{c_{2}}\circ L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{T}_{0,f}\circ u_{c_{1},!}
.

•

For every object
c\in\mathscr{C}
denote by
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
the essential image of
\mathscr{E}_{c}
under
u_{!}
. Then
u_{c}^{*}
takes
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
to
\mathscr{E}_{c}
and moreover the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{T}_{0,c},\mathscr{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}(\mathscr{T}_{0,c},\mathscr{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\mathrm{comp}}i_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{c}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{c}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{c}}

is a pullback square.

•

For every object
c\in\mathscr{C}
the monad
\widetilde{t}^{*}_{c}\widetilde{t}_{c,!}
respects
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
, where
\widetilde{t}:L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{T}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{1}
is the image of
t
under
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}
.

We will denote by
\mathrm{Th}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathscr{C})
the full subcategory on good
\mathscr{C}
-theories. Observe that every complete theory is good.

Remark 6.8
.

It follows from
[
CH19
, Corollary 13.4.]
and
[
CH19
, Corollary 13.8.]
that the theory of
6.6
is good.

Theorem 6.9
.

The natural inclusion
i_{\mathrm{comp}}:\mathrm{Th}^{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathscr{C})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Th}^{\mathrm{good}}(\mathscr{C})
admits a left adjoint
L^{\mathrm{comp}}
. Moreover, there is an isomorphism
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{S})\cong\mathrm{Mod}_{L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{S})
for every good
\mathscr{C}
-theory
\mathscr{T}
.

Proof.

We define the underlying
\mathrm{Cat}^{\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}}_{/\mathscr{C}}
-object of
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{T}
to be given by
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}(\mathscr{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{t}\mathscr{T}_{1})
. Observe that to provide the left adjoint it now suffices to endow
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}(\mathscr{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{t}\mathscr{T}_{1})
with the structure of a theory and the natural morphism
u:(\mathscr{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{t}\mathscr{T}_{1})\rightarrow L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}(\mathscr{T}_{0}\xrightarrow{t}\mathscr{T}_{1})
with the structure of a morphism in
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{act}}
. For every
c\in\mathscr{C}
we will denote by
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
the essential image of
\mathscr{E}_{c}
under
u_{c,!}
. We need to show that this actually gives us the object of
\mathrm{Th}(\mathscr{C})
. The fact that
\widetilde{t}^{*}_{c}\widetilde{t}_{c,!}
respects
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
follows directly from
6.7
. Now we need to prove that for every
f:c\rightarrow c^{\prime}
the correspondence
L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{T}_{0,f}:L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}:\mathscr{T}_{0,c}\rightarrow L^{\mathrm{i.o.}}\mathscr{T}_{0,c^{\prime}}
takes
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c}
to
L^{\mathrm{comp}}\mathscr{E}_{c^{\prime}}
. To do this it suffices to prove that the following diagram in
\mathrm{Corr}
commutes

Observe that, since

, it suffices to prove that

, which is a part of
6.7
. This proves that

indeed belongs to

thus finishing the proof of the first claim.

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The outer square in this diagram is a pullback by the definition of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{T}}(\mathscr{S})
, the lower square is a pullback by
6.7
, so it follows that the top square is also a pullback, which is exactly what we needed to prove.
∎
